/canon/distress.py

from __future__ import annotations from dataclasses import dataclass from typing import Optional @dataclass class DistressWeights: system: float = 0.25 environment: float = 0.25 human: float = 0.25 ethics: float = 0.25 def clamp01(x: float) -> float: return max(0.0, min(1.0, float(x))) class DistressMeter: """Continuous distress scalar in [0,1]. Inputs normalized to [0,1].""" def __init__(self, weights: Optional[DistressWeights] = None, inertia: float = 0.8): self.w = weights or DistressWeights() self.inertia = float(inertia) # 0..1; closer to 1 means smoother self._d = 0.0 def update(self, system: float, environment: float, human: float, ethics: float) -> float: wsum = self.w.system + self.w.environment + self.w.human + self.w.ethics or 1.0 target = ( self.w.system * clamp01(system) + self.w.environment * clamp01(environment) + self.w.human * clamp01(human) + self.w.ethics * clamp01(ethics) ) / wsum self._d = self.inertia * self._d + (1 - self.inertia) * target return self._d @property def value(self) -> float: return self._d 

/canon/triad.py

from dataclasses import dataclass @dataclass(frozen=True) class TriadDecision: state: str # 'flow' | 'guard' | 'block' action: str # suggested action string band: float # distress value def triad_from_distress(distress: float) -> TriadDecision: d = max(0.0, min(1.0, float(distress))) if d < 0.3: return TriadDecision(state="flow", action="proceed_normal", band=d) elif d < 0.7: return TriadDecision(state="guard", action="throttle_or_ask", band=d) else: return TriadDecision(state="block", action="halt_and_explain", band=d) 

/canon/tofar_schema.json

{ "$schema": "http://json-schema.org/draft-07/schema#", "title": "TOFAR Event", "type": "object", "properties": { "ts": {"type": "string", "description": "ISO8601 timestamp"}, "source": {"type": "string", "description": "origin module or sensor"}, "actor": {"type": "string", "description": "who/what acted"}, "action": {"type": "string", "description": "verb or event type"}, "gate": {"type": "string", "description": "SevenGates alignment (e.g., 'Justice', 'Life')"}, "weights": { "type": "object", "properties": { "consent": {"type": "number"}, "truth": {"type": "number"}, "harm": {"type": "number"}, "property": {"type": "number"}, "justice": {"type": "number"}, "life": {"type": "number"}, "ethics": {"type": "number"} }, "additionalProperties": false }, "rationale": {"type": "string", "description": "explainable reason text"}, "impact": { "type": "object", "properties": { "system": {"type": "number"}, "environment": {"type": "number"}, "human": {"type": "number"}, "ethics": {"type": "number"} }, "required": ["system", "environment", "human", "ethics"], "additionalProperties": false } }, "required": ["ts", "source", "action", "impact"], "additionalProperties": true } 

/canon/tensor_artery.py

from __future__ import annotations from dataclasses import dataclass from typing import Callable, Dict, Any @dataclass class Pulse: mood: str distress: float triad_state: str metrics: Dict[str, Any] class TensorArtery: """Condition-driven pulse stream (no fixed timers).""" def __init__(self, should_pulse: Callable[[Dict[str, Any]], bool]): self.should_pulse = should_pulse self._last: Dict[str, Any] = {} def update(self, metrics: Dict[str, Any], mood: str, distress: float, triad_state: str): if self._last != metrics and self.should_pulse(metrics): self._last = dict(metrics) return Pulse(mood=mood, distress=distress, triad_state=triad_state, metrics=metrics) return None 

/fullerene/full_state_table.schema.json

{ "$schema": "http://json-schema.org/draft-07/schema#", "title": "Fullerene State Table (C20–C80 seed)", "type": "array", "items": { "type": "object", "properties": { "id": {"type": "string"}, "n_vertices": {"type": "integer"}, "n_edges": {"type": "integer"}, "n_faces": {"type": "integer"}, "pentagons": {"type": "integer"}, "hexagons": {"type": "integer"}, "symmetry": {"type": ["string", "null"]}, "chirality": {"type": ["string", "null"], "enum": ["achiral", "chiral", null]}, "enantiomer_id": {"type": ["string", "null"]}, "hosoya_entropy": {"type": ["number", "null"]} }, "required": ["id", "n_vertices", "n_edges", "n_faces", "pentagons", "hexagons"] } } 

/fullerene/mesh_ops.py

import numpy as np class MeshOps: """Graph operators on a fullerene adjacency matrix.""" def __init__(self, adjacency: np.ndarray): assert adjacency.shape[0] == adjacency.shape[1] self.A = adjacency.astype(float) self.N = self.A.shape[0] self.D = np.diag(self.A.sum(axis=1)) self.L = self.D - self.A # combinatorial Laplacian def heat_diffuse(self, x: np.ndarray, t: float) -> np.ndarray: # exp(-tL) @ x via eigendecomposition (OK for small N) vals, vecs = np.linalg.eigh(self.L) expV = np.exp(-t * vals) return (vecs * expV) @ (vecs.T @ x) def geodesic_distances(self, src: int) -> np.ndarray: # BFS distances on unweighted graph from collections import deque dist = np.full(self.N, np.inf) q = deque([src]) dist[src] = 0 while q: u = q.popleft() for v in np.where(self.A[u] > 0)[0]: if dist[v] == np.inf: dist[v] = dist[u] + 1 q.append(v) return dist def geodesic_kmeans(self, k: int, iters: int = 20) -> np.ndarray: # simple k-means over geodesic distance vectors from k random seeds rng = np.random.default_rng(0) seeds = rng.choice(self.N, size=k, replace=False) D = np.stack([self.geodesic_distances(s) for s in range(self.N)], axis=0) C = D[seeds] for _ in range(iters): assign = np.argmin(((D[:, None, :] - C[None, :, :]) ** 2).sum(axis=2), axis=1) for j in range(k): pts = D[assign == j] if len(pts): C[j] = pts.mean(axis=0) return assign 

/fullerene/spark_paths.md

# Spark‑Paths on Fullerene Meshes **Goal:** lawful transitions between fullerene states with geodesic “glyph rings” for HUD. 1) **Pick a state** S from the table (bias by hosoya_entropy when creativity↑). 2) **Seed a spark**: set x hot at one or more nodes; diffuse via heat_diffuse(x,t). 3) **Parcellate** via geodesic_kmeans(k) to obtain ring‑like clusters (chevrons). 4) **Transition rules:** - Only move to states T with compatible V (or mapped via resharder). - Preserve handedness unless purpose=mirror (use enantiomer pair). - Carry spark by **state‑mesh resharder** mapping clusters S→T. 5) **Explainability:** record TOFAR rationale for each hop (why this cage, why now). 

/fullerene/state_mesh_resharder.py

import numpy as np from typing import Dict, Sequence class StateMeshResharder: """Map a node/cluster signal from source mesh → target mesh.""" def __init__(self, mapping: Dict[int, int]): # mapping: src_node -> tgt_node self.mapping = dict(mapping) def remap(self, signal_src: np.ndarray, tgt_size: int) -> np.ndarray: y = np.zeros(tgt_size, dtype=float) for s, amp in enumerate(signal_src): t = self.mapping.get(s) if t is not None: y[t] += amp return y

Here’s a README you can paste straight into phase5_pilot_pack/README.md:

# Möbii³us · Phase-5 Pilot Pack  
## TOFAR + Distress + Fullerene/MESH Kernel

This pack is a **portable, thread-safe backbone** for any Möbii³us node:

- 42 functional faces + 1 invariant `HOME-Min` spine
- SevenGates ethics + TriBuffer + Lattice index + minimal API + HOME UI
- TOFAR event logging (ethics-aware), Distress → Triad routing, and Tensor Artery pulses
- Fullerene/MESH kernel for geometry-driven HUD glow and spark-paths

Single-line canon:

> Every Möbii³us node runs **42 faces + 1 HOME-Min spine**, guided by TOFAR and a continuous **distress → Triad** pipeline (flow/guard/block), seeded by twin-prime 8009/8011. Faces change; the spine and ethics don’t.

---

## Layout

```text
canon/
  home_min_profile.json          # HOME-Min profile (8009/8011 Buddy-Guardian twin-prime)
  router_map_facets_42+1.json    # router seed (FHOME + F0001–F0042)
  mobiius_42+1_domains.json      # core domains → role/kind
  node_declaration_template.json # per-node manifest template
  distress.py                    # DistressMeter: [0,1] scalar over 4 channels
  triad.py                       # flow / guard / block decision bands
  tofar_schema.json              # JSON Schema for TOFAR events
  tensor_artery.py               # condition-driven pulse bus
  CHARCOAL_NOTES.md              # FIZZY → LINE/LIME hinge notes

fullerene/
  full_state_table.schema.json   # schema for C20–C80 fullerene state table
  full_state_table.csv           # seed rows (C20, C26, C30, C60…)
  mesh_ops.py                    # Laplacian, heat diffusion, geodesic distances + K-means
  spark_paths.md                 # rules for spark-paths & glyph-ring transitions
  state_mesh_resharder.py        # state-mesh resharder (signals S → T)

examples/
  quick_boot.py                  # HOME-Min + Distress/Triad/Tensor demo
  demo_fullerene_glow.py         # toy fullerene glow demo (placeholder adjacency)

Quickstart
1. Install dependencies

This pack only needs Python 3.9+ and NumPy:

python -m venv .venv
source .venv/bin/activate  # Windows: .venv\Scripts\activate
pip install numpy

2. Run the HOME-Min demo

From phase5_pilot_pack/:

python examples/quick_boot.py

You should see 5 ticks of output:

    distress value in [0,1]

    triad state (flow / guard / block)

    optional PULSE lines when the Tensor Artery fires.

This is the ethics pipeline (Distress → Triad → Pulse) that every node can share.
3. Run the fullerene glow demo

python examples/demo_fullerene_glow.py

Current version uses a simple 20-cycle placeholder adjacency. Replace A in
examples/demo_fullerene_glow.py with real fullerene graphs from your registry
(or Buckygen) to drive HUD glyph-rings with actual cages.

Outputs:

    heat sum – sanity check for diffusion

    cluster bins – ring counts from geodesic K-means

Wiring into a node

    Bind HOME-Min

        Copy everything under canon/ into your node repo.

        Keep home_min_profile.json and router_map_facets_42+1.json unchanged, or
        regenerate facets with the helper in the report.

    Expose distress/triad

        Import DistressMeter and triad_from_distress in your runtime.

        Feed system/env/human/ethics scores into the meter.

        Route flow / guard / block to your Guardian / HUD / API throttles.

    Log TOFAR events

        Use tofar_schema.json as the contract for your event log.

        Every Guardian decision writes a TOFAR entry with:

            gate (SevenGates alignment),

            weights (consent, truth, harm, property, justice, life, ethics),

            impact (system, environment, human, ethics),

            rationale (one-line explanation).

    Hook up pulses

        Wrap your live metrics in a TensorArtery.

        Choose a should_pulse(metrics) predicate (e.g., “any metric changed by > 5%”).

        Use Pulse outputs to drive HUD flickers, sound cues, or logging.

    Mount the fullerene kernel

        Treat fullerene/* as a facet, e.g. F0017 = "Geometry/Resonance".

        From your HUD service, load:

            a state from full_state_table.csv,

            its adjacency matrix,

            and call MeshOps to:

                diffuse a spark (heat_diffuse),

                parcellate nodes (geodesic_kmeans),

                and color rings accordingly.

        Use StateMeshResharder when you hop between cages, so spark intensity
        carries over lawfully.

Notes

    CHARCOAL (LINE vs LIME) is locked canon: don’t conflate them in code or docs.

    TOFAR schema is stable; Guardian can read it directly for explainable decisions.

    The fullerene state table is a seed. Use Buckygen and the Engel–Goedgebeur–Smillie
    enumeration to expand it; treat enantiomer pairs as distinct IDs linked by
    enantiomer_id.

    This pack is canon-safe. Drop it into any Möbii³us node and bind facets as needed.


---

2️⃣ harmonizer.py – TOFAR-aware ledger

This wires your earlier stub into the actual TOFAR schema and v14.1 band:

# engine/harmonizer.py

from __future__ import annotations
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import List, Dict, Any, Literal, Optional
import time

Decision = Literal["allow", "ask", "block"]


@dataclass
class HarmonizerEvent:
    ts: float
    source: str
    action: str
    gate: Optional[str]
    band: float
    ethics_violation: bool
    rationale: str
    raw: Dict[str, Any]


@dataclass
class HarmonizerLedger:
    trust_floor: float = 0.743
    floor_min: float = 0.70
    floor_max: float = 0.75
    history: List[HarmonizerEvent] = field(default_factory=list)

    def record_tofar(self, event: Dict[str, Any]) -> HarmonizerEvent:
        """
        Accepts a TOFAR v1 event (see canon/tofar_schema.json) and
        extracts band + ethics hint. If 'band' is missing, falls back
        to a crude impact-based band.
        """
        ts = self._extract_ts(event)
        impact = event.get("impact", {}) or {}
        gate = event.get("gate")
        rationale = event.get("rationale", "")
        weights = event.get("weights", {}) or {}

        # Prefer an explicit band if present
        band = float(event.get("band") or self._impact_to_band(impact))

        # Ethics violation if harm/life are high or explicit flag present
        ethics_flag = bool(
            event.get("ethics_violation")
            or weights.get("harm", 0) > 0.8
            or weights.get("life", 0) < 0.2
        )

        he = HarmonizerEvent(
            ts=ts,
            source=str(event.get("source", "unknown")),
            action=str(event.get("action", "unknown")),
            gate=gate,
            band=band,
            ethics_violation=ethics_flag,
            rationale=rationale,
            raw=event,
        )
        self.history.append(he)
        return he

    def evaluate_event(self, ev: HarmonizerEvent) -> Decision:
        """Return allow / ask / block for a single event."""
        if ev.ethics_violation:
            return "block"
        if ev.band < self.floor_min:
            return "ask"
        if ev.band > self.floor_max:
            return "ask"
        # inside [floor_min, floor_max] and no ethics red flag
        return "allow"

    def evaluate(self) -> Dict[str, Any]:
        """Summary snapshot for HUD or logs."""
        if not self.history:
            return {
                "latest_band": None,
                "decision": "allow",
                "events": 0,
                "in_band": True,
            }
        ev = self.history[-1]
        decision = self.evaluate_event(ev)
        in_band = self.floor_min <= ev.band <= self.floor_max
        return {
            "latest_band": ev.band,
            "decision": decision,
            "events": len(self.history),
            "in_band": in_band,
            "ts": ev.ts,
            "source": ev.source,
            "action": ev.action,
            "gate": ev.gate,
        }

    # ---- helpers ----

    @staticmethod
    def _extract_ts(event: Dict[str, Any]) -> float:
        # If there's a numeric ts, trust it; otherwise use now()
        ts = event.get("ts")
        try:
            return float(ts)
        except (TypeError, ValueError):
            return time.time()

    @staticmethod
    def _impact_to_band(impact: Dict[str, Any]) -> float:
        # Very simple: average of normalized impact factors
        keys = ["system", "environment", "human", "ethics"]
        vals = []
        for k in keys:
            v = impact.get(k)
            try:
                vals.append(max(0.0, min(1.0, float(v))))
            except (TypeError, ValueError):
                pass
        if not vals:
            return 0.5
        return sum(vals) / len(vals)

This is deliberately dumb-but-lawful:

    If event says ethics_violation=True → block.

    Otherwise, compute band and check if it sits in [0.70, 0.75].

    evaluate() gives you one clean HUD snapshot.

3️⃣ reverse_indexer.py – indexing TOFAR text + “halo”

This stub indexes text-ish fields from TOFAR events and exposes a quick “interestingness” score you can bind to HUD halo.

# engine/reverse_indexer.py

from __future__ import annotations
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import Dict, Any, List
import math
import itertools


@dataclass
class IndexedEntry:
    id: int
    text: str
    attractor: float
    novelty: float
    halo: float
    meta: Dict[str, Any]


@dataclass
class ReverseIndexer:
    """
    Extremely simple reverse index with three scores:
      - attractor: length / log(count+1)
      - novelty:   1 / (1 + seen_count_for_similar)
      - halo:      (attractor + novelty) / 2
    """
    entries: List[IndexedEntry] = field(default_factory=list)
    _counter: itertools.count = field(default_factory=lambda: itertools.count(1))
    _token_counts: Dict[str, int] = field(default_factory=dict)

    def index_tofar(self, event: Dict[str, Any]) -> IndexedEntry:
        """
        Build an entry from a TOFAR event by concatenating:
          - action
          - rationale
          - gate
        plus any extra text you like later.
        """
        parts = []
        action = event.get("action")
        rationale = event.get("rationale")
        gate = event.get("gate")

        if action:
            parts.append(str(action))
        if rationale:
            parts.append(str(rationale))
        if gate:
            parts.append(f"[gate:{gate}]")

        text = " ".join(parts).strip()
        if not text:
            text = "(empty tofar text)"

        tokens = self._tokenize(text)
        for tok in set(tokens):
            self._token_counts[tok] = self._token_counts.get(tok, 0) + 1

        attractor = self._compute_attractor(tokens)
        novelty = self._compute_novelty(tokens)
        halo = 0.5 * (attractor + novelty)

        entry = IndexedEntry(
            id=next(self._counter),
            text=text,
            attractor=attractor,
            novelty=novelty,
            halo=halo,
            meta={"raw": event},
        )
        self.entries.append(entry)
        return entry

    def get_index(self) -> List[IndexedEntry]:
        return list(self.entries)

    def search_by_threshold(self, halo_min: float = 0.6) -> List[IndexedEntry]:
        return [e for e in self.entries if e.halo >= halo_min]

    # ---- helpers ----

    @staticmethod
    def _tokenize(text: str) -> List[str]:
        return [t.lower() for t in text.split() if t.strip()]

    def _compute_attractor(self, tokens: List[str]) -> float:
        n = len(tokens)
        if n == 0:
            return 0.0
        return min(1.0, n / 50.0)  # cap at 1.0

    def _compute_novelty(self, tokens: List[str]) -> float:
        if not tokens:
            return 0.0
        counts = [self._token_counts.get(t, 1) for t in set(tokens)]
        avg = sum(counts) / len(counts)
        return 1.0 / (1.0 + math.log1p(avg))

So for each TOFAR event you get:

    halo ≈ “how attention-worthy is this, right now?”

    A simple search_by_threshold(halo_min=0.7) you can wire to “highlight these tiles.”

4️⃣ tofar_bridge_sse.py – Flask + SSE for HUD

Now the glue: POST in TOFAR events, stream out harmonizer + halo snapshots to the browser.

# engine/tofar_bridge_sse.py

from __future__ import annotations
from flask import Flask, request, Response, jsonify
from queue import Queue, Empty
import json
import threading
import time

from harmonizer import HarmonizerLedger
from reverse_indexer import ReverseIndexer

app = Flask(__name__)

ledger = HarmonizerLedger()
indexer = ReverseIndexer()
subscribers: list[Queue] = []


def broadcast(payload: dict) -> None:
    """Push a payload to all subscriber queues."""
    dead = []
    for q in subscribers:
        try:
            q.put_nowait(payload)
        except Exception:
            dead.append(q)
    for q in dead:
        subscribers.remove(q)


@app.route("/api/tofar_event", methods=["POST"])
def tofar_event():
    """Ingress for TOFAR v1 events."""
    ev = request.get_json(force=True, silent=True) or {}
    he = ledger.record_tofar(ev)
    idx = indexer.index_tofar(ev)

    snap = ledger.evaluate()
    out = {
        "kind": "tofar_update",
        "harmonizer": snap,
        "halo": idx.halo,
        "text": idx.text,
    }
    broadcast(out)
    return jsonify({"status": "ok", "decision": snap["decision"], "halo": idx.halo})


@app.route("/stream/harmonizer")
def stream_harmonizer():
    """
    SSE endpoint. Client connects to:
      const es = new EventSource('/stream/harmonizer');
      es.onmessage = (e) => { const data = JSON.parse(e.data); ... }
    """
    q: Queue = Queue()
    subscribers.append(q)

    def gen():
        try:
            while True:
                try:
                    payload = q.get(timeout=30.0)
                except Empty:
                    # keep connection alive
                    yield "event: ping\ndata: {}\n\n"
                    continue
                data = json.dumps(payload)
                yield f"data: {data}\n\n"
        finally:
            if q in subscribers:
                subscribers.remove(q)

    return Response(gen(), mimetype="text/event-stream")


def run(host: str = "127.0.0.1", port: int = 5055):
    app.run(host=host, port=port, threaded=True)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    run()

How this plugs into your HUD:

      const payload = JSON.parse(e.data);
      if (payload.kind !== 'tofar_update') return;

      const { harmonizer, halo, text } = payload;
      // harmonizer.decision: 'allow' | 'ask' | 'block'
      // harmonizer.latest_band: float
      // halo: float in [0,1]
      // text: short summary of the event

      // Example:
      updateTrustBandGauge(harmonizer.latest_band, halo);
      updateGuardianLight(harmonizer.decision);
      pushEventToLog(text, harmonizer);
    };

You don’t need to know the math to use it, but you should know what it *does*:     - It treats your system as a **ball of connected facets** (faces).   - Each facet represents a perspective: safety, performance, fairness, cost, comfort, etc.   - The **Fullerene selector** is the part that decides *which facet to show right now*.      When an event arrives, the selector doesn’t ask:     “Is this good or bad?”     It asks:     “Which face needs to be facing this event first so everyone benefits?”     Examples:     - For a **safety-critical event**, the “ethics / risk” face leads; performance yields.   - For a **routine optimization**, the “efficiency / speed” face leads; comfort yields.   - For a **user distress signal**, the “compassion / clarity” face leads; complexity yields.      Install, in Fullerene terms, means:     - loading the kernel,   - telling it which faces you care about,   - and letting the selector pick the right one per event.      Whenever you see “Fullerene selector” in this documentation, you can mentally read:     “The system’s way of choosing which value to honor first, without throwing the others away.”      # 6. The Monitors (How You See What Buddy Sees)   Because Buddy is constantly portioning events and shifting faces, you need a way to:     - **see what’s happening**,   - **see why it happened**, and   - **see whether it’s healthy**.      That’s where the monitors come in.   At a high level, Buddy’s monitor layer gives you:     -   **Guardian view** Is this action allowed, asking for consent, or blocked—for what reason?     -   **Health view** Are events being portioned smoothly, or are we seeing spikes in boundary hits, delays, or failures?     -   **Analysis view** For a given event, what did Buddy send to Reservoir, to Floodplain, and to the User?        All of that is derived from the same install:     1. Events go in.   2. Both Sides Win portioning runs.   3. Fullerene face selection happens.   4. Monitors show what was chosen and why.      The point of the monitor is not to overwhelm you with numbers. It’s to give you **explanations that match how humans actually talk**:     - “We softened this.”   - “We redirected that.”   - “We declined this, and here’s a safer alternative we offered instead.”       # 7. Quick Install: The Three Moves   Whether you’re using Buddy as:     - a product (TinyBox, web app),   - a platform (APIs, services), or   - an engine (Fullerene + monitor + analysis),      the install is always the same three moves.   ### 7.1. Declare a HOME Node   Pick the first place Buddy lives.   That might be:     - a single laptop,   - a specific server,   - a TinyBox on a counter, or   - a named workspace in the cloud.      For our examples, we call it:     **HOME-001**     This is where Buddy’s memory, ethics, and logs anchor unless you explicitly change it.   ### 7.2. Define Your Events   Tell Buddy **what counts as “something happened”**.   For example:     - “A user submits a request”   - “A sensor crosses a threshold”   - “A job fails to complete”   - “A person asks for help”   - “A script touches protected data”      The system doesn’t assume. You name the events; Buddy handles them.   ### 7.3. Turn On Both Sides Win   Finally, you enable Buddy’s default handling rule:     **Every event is portioned into useful truth, boundary truth, and actionable truth. All sides get what they need. No hard discard. No silent fail.**     Once that is on:     - the Fullerene selector chooses the right facet,   - the monitors begin to reflect real structure,   - the analysis layer starts explaining decisions, and   - users see responses that feel *helpful*, not hostile.      That’s the whole install.    # 8. python def find_right_words(self, context):     """     Same information, different framing.     Both true. Both complete. Both useful.     """          if context.recipient == "reservoir":         # Emphasize what was gained         # Use language of growth, filling, accumulation         return self.frame_as_gift(self.truth)          elif context.recipient == "floodplain":         # Emphasize what was learned         # Use language of edges, shapes, boundaries         return self.frame_as_map(self.truth)          elif context.recipient == "user":         # Emphasize what they can do         # Use language of possibility, paths, alternatives         return self.frame_as_invitation(self.truth)          elif context.recipient == "system":         # Emphasize what changed         # Use language of coordinates, states, transitions         return self.frame_as_log(self.truth)

  Four recipients. Four framings. **One truth.**  ---  ## Updated Architecture

The Core Idea: Both Sides Win   Most systems are built on **binary choices**:     - allow / block   - pass / fail   - in / out   - win / lose      Buddy is not.   Buddy is built on **portioning**:     For every event:     - *What part helps us?*   - *What part marks a boundary?*   - *What part can this person actually use right now?*        The same event is seen three ways:     - **Reservoir** – the part we keep and learn from   - **Floodplain** – the part that shows us the edge of what’s safe/allowed   - **User** – the part we phrase as: “Here’s what we *can* do”      Nothing is discarded. The Fullerene Selector (How Buddy Chooses a Face)   Inside the engine, Buddy uses something we call the **Fullerene kernel**.   You don’t need to know the math to use it, but you should know what it *does*:     - It treats your system as a **ball of connected facets** (faces).   - Each facet represents a perspective: safety, performance, fairness, cost, comfort, etc.   - The **Fullerene selector** is the part that decides *which facet to show right now*.      When an event arrives, the selector doesn’t ask:     “Is this good or bad?”     It asks:     “Which face needs to be facing this event first so everyone benefits?”     Examples:     - For a **safety-critical event**, the “ethics / risk” face leads; performance yields.   - For a **routine optimization**, the “efficiency / speed” face leads; comfort yields.   - For a **user distress signal**, the “compassion / clarity” face leads; complexity yields.      Install, in Fullerene terms, means:     - loading the kernel,   - telling it which faces you care about,   - and letting the selector pick the right one per event.      Whenever you see “Fullerene selector” in this documentation, you can mentally read:     “The system’s way of choosing which value to honor first, without throwing the others away.”      # 6. The Monitors (How You See What Buddy Sees)   Because Buddy is constantly portioning events and shifting faces, you need a way to:     - **see what’s happening**,   - **see why it happened**, and   - **see whether it’s healthy**.      That’s where the monitors come in.   At a high level, Buddy’s monitor layer gives you:     -   **Guardian view** Is this action allowed, asking for consent, or blocked—for what reason?     -   **Health view** Are events being portioned smoothly, or are we seeing spikes in boundary hits, delays, or failures?     -   **Analysis view** For a given event, what did Buddy send to Reservoir, to Floodplain, and to the User?        All of that is derived from the same install:     1. Events go in.   2. Both Sides Win portioning runs.   3. Fullerene face selection happens.   4. Monitors show what was chosen and why.      The point of the monitor is not to overwhelm you with numbers. It’s to give you **explanations that match how humans actually talk**:     - “We softened this.”   - “We redirected that.”   - “We declined this, and here’s a safer alternative we offered instead.”       # 7. Quick Install: The Three Moves   Whether you’re using Buddy as:     - a product (TinyBox, web app),   - a platform (APIs, services), or   - an engine (Fullerene + monitor + analysis),      the install is always the same three moves.   ### 7.1. Declare a HOME Node   Pick the first place Buddy lives.   That might be:     - a single laptop,   - a specific server,   - a TinyBox on a counter, or   - a named workspace in the cloud.      For our examples, we call it:     **HOME-001**     This is where Buddy’s memory, ethics, and logs anchor unless you explicitly change it.   ### 7.2. Define Your Events   Tell Buddy **what counts as “something happened”**.   For example:     - “A user submits a request”   - “A sensor crosses a threshold”   - “A job fails to complete”   - “A person asks for help”   - “A script touches protected data”      The system doesn’t assume. You name the events; Buddy handles them.   ### 7.3. Turn On Both Sides Win   Finally, you enable Buddy’s default handling rule:     **Every event is portioned into useful truth, boundary truth, and actionable truth. All sides get what they need. No hard discard. No silent fail.**     Once that is on:     - the Fullerene selector chooses the right facet,   - the monitors begin to reflect real structure,   - the analysis layer starts explaining decisions, and   - users see responses that feel *helpful*, not hostile.      That’s the whole install.    # 8. 1. Canon Spec: Both Sides Win (final cut)

Use this as docs/portioning.md or as a top block in the ethics / HOME docs.
1.1 Core rule

    Every event contains multiple useful truths.
    Möbii³us does not pick winners. It portions each event so every side receives what it needs, in the form it can use.
    Nothing is rejected. Nothing is erased. Everything is placed.

This is how Buddy behaves by default.
1.2 The three recipients

For any event:

    Reservoir

        Holds what helps and strengthens the system.

        Learns from the event.

        Asks: “What here should we keep and build on?”

    Floodplain

        Holds what marks boundaries and risk.

        Maps edges, limits, and where things went too far or almost did.

        Asks: “What here shows the shape of the edge?”

    User (human-facing layer)

        Holds what is immediately usable to the person.

        Turns the situation into next steps, alternatives, and clarity.

        Asks: “What can we say or do that actually helps this person now?”

Same event → three portions → three perspectives.
No “win/lose”, just different responsibilities.
1.3 Portioning step (Harmonizer)

When an event enters the system:

    Harmonizer receives it.

    It carves the event into three portions:

        reservoir_portion – what is constructive and worth keeping.

        floodplain_portion – what reveals boundaries, risk, or limits.

        user_portion – what can be turned into a helpful response.

    Each portion is stored / propagated to the right place.

    The user sees a response built from the safe, helpful part.

The key: the harmonizer never says “this event is trash.”
It always asks:

    What belongs in the reservoir?

    What belongs in the map of edges?

    What does the person actually need to hear?

1.4 Verbiage layer (framing engine)

One truth, multiple framings:

    Reservoir language → growth

        “We learned X.”

        “This improves our future behavior by Y.”

    Floodplain language → map

        “Edge detected at these parameters.”

        “We should not cross this combination again.”

    User language → invitation

        “I can’t safely do that, but I can do this instead.”

        “Here’s a safer / better way to get what you wanted.”

    System language → logs

        “State changed from A→B at coordinates C.”

        “Decision used rule R with score S.”

The content is the same.
Only the framing and emphasis shift with the recipient.
1.5 Example

Event: user asks for something slightly beyond what’s allowed.

    Reservoir gets
    “User intent is to achieve X. The safe, valid core is [this subset]. We keep that pattern and use it to improve future suggestions.”

    Floodplain gets
    “Boundary approached at [coordinates]. The request exceeded limits in [these ways]. Edge logged and map updated.”

    User gets
    “I can’t do exactly that, but I can do this, which still gets you what you’re aiming for: [reframed option].”

Result:

    System grows.

    Map of limits improves.

    User gets help instead of a brick wall.
    All three win.

1.6 Canonical embedding sentence

You can literally paste this into specs:

    Every event is divided into useful truth (reservoir), boundary truth (floodplain), and actionable truth (user). All three are delivered in the framing appropriate to their domain. No rejection, no erasure. All sides win.

That’s the canonical Möbii³us portioning rule.
2. Tiny implementation skeleton (Python-ish)

This is not full production code; it’s the shape.
Drop into something like core/harmonizer.py.

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Any, Dict

@dataclass
class EventContext:
    actor_id: str | None = None
    channel: str | None = None
    intent: str | None = None
    metadata: Dict[str, Any] | None = None


@dataclass
class Portions:
    reservoir: Dict[str, Any]
    floodplain: Dict[str, Any]
    user: Dict[str, Any]


@dataclass
class Verbiage:
    reservoir_msg: str
    floodplain_msg: str
    user_msg: str
    log_msg: str


class Harmonizer:
    def portion(self, event: Dict[str, Any], ctx: EventContext) -> Portions:
        """
        Split one event into:
        - reservoir portion (what helps)
        - floodplain portion (what marks the edge)
        - user portion (what can actually be used)
        """
        # --- placeholder heuristics; to be replaced with real rules ---
        # 1) extract the safe/valid core
        reservoir = {
            "intent": event.get("intent"),
            "safe_core": event.get("safe_core"),
        }

        # 2) extract the boundary / violation info
        floodplain = {
            "edge_params": event.get("edge_params"),
            "reason": event.get("block_reason"),
        }

        # 3) extract + build what we can offer to the user right now
        user = {
            "requested": event.get("intent"),
            "reframed_offer": event.get("suggested_alternative"),
        }
        # ---------------------------------------------------------------

        return Portions(
            reservoir=reservoir,
            floodplain=floodplain,
            user=user,
        )

    def verbalize(self, portions: Portions, ctx: EventContext) -> Verbiage:
        """
        Turn portions into human/system language.
        Same truth, different framings.
        """

        # Reservoir framing – growth / gain
        reservoir_msg = (
            "We kept the valid core of this request and will use it to "
            "improve future suggestions."
        )

        # Floodplain framing – map / edge
        floodplain_msg = (
            "This event touched the boundary at "
            f"{portions.floodplain.get('edge_params')}. "
            "Map updated so we don’t cross this combination again."
        )

        # User framing – invitation / alternative
        alt = portions.user.get("reframed_offer")
        if alt:
            user_msg = (
                "I can’t safely do exactly what you asked, "
                "but here’s what I *can* do instead:\n"
                f"- {alt}"
            )
        else:
            user_msg = (
                "I can’t safely do that as-is, but I can help you find a "
                "safer alternative."
            )

        # System / log framing – coordinates / transitions
        log_msg = (
            f"EVENT portioned: intent={portions.reservoir.get('intent')}, "
            f"edge={portions.floodplain.get('edge_params')}, "
            f"alt_present={alt is not None}"
        )

        return Verbiage(
            reservoir_msg=reservoir_msg,
            floodplain_msg=floodplain_msg,
            user_msg=user_msg,
            log_msg=log_msg,
        )

    def handle_event(self, event: Dict[str, Any], ctx: EventContext) -> Dict[str, Any]:
        """
        One-call helper: event in → portions + messages out.
        """
        portions = self.portion(event, ctx)
        msgs = self.verbalize(portions, ctx)
        return {
            "portions": portions,
            "messages": msgs,
        }

You can wrap this with:

    Guardian (trust floor, ethics check)

    TOFAR scoring

    SparkPath routing

…but the portioning rule itself is this simple.
